- **PR-MED-026** (MED, behavioral, `scripts/probe-cliniko.py:862`): Restore can overwrite a new answer in either test question even when the markers are not the only difference. If the practitioner changes a targeted answer during the final pause while other answers match their originals, the guard passes, overwrites that edit and reports success. This violates Flow 4’s marker-only restoration condition. — Evidence: the comparison explicitly excludes targets with `"if p not in targets"` (865), then executes `self._patch(original, "restore")` (877). Recommendation: Fix-now — Check targeted answers against the original or expected marker-only state before restoring; refuse unexpected edits with `MARKER LEFT` and add coverage for edits to each target during the final pause. /fix decision: Applied (as NARROWED by LEG 1: restore kept, report added) — `_restore(original, targets, rich)` prints, for each test question, "held only a marker or its original answer: yes/no". `_marker_or_original` compares visible text against the original answer and both markers. The restore condition stays "structure unchanged and every UNTARGETED question as at the start", recorded as AS-BUILT in Flow 4 and Task 1.2. Test: `test_a_test_question_edited_before_the_restore_is_restored_and_reported`, plus two lines in the full run. /fix notes: `scripts/probe-cliniko.py`, `desktop/tests/test_probe_cliniko.py`, plan Flow 4 / Task 1.2; siblings none. Verified: ruff and mypy clean in-leg; suite composer-run. /fix date: 2026-09-29. /fix applied by: Claude Code
- **PR-MED-027** (MED, behavioral, `scripts/probe-cliniko.py:836`): An ignored partial PATCH can be reported as `MERGED`. The partial pass sends the same marker already installed by the full pass and judges success solely by preservation of other questions. A server returning 200 without applying the partial body therefore produces the same observations as a successful merge, potentially giving Task 2.1 false evidence for selecting partial bodies. — Evidence: `body = partial_content(first, rich, targets[rich])` (784); the comparison excludes the target with `"if p != rich"` (831); `verdict = "MERGED" if untouched else ...` (836). Recommendation: Fix-now — Send a distinguishable marker for the partial pass and require its read-back at the intended target before reporting a merge; cover a 200 response that ignores the partial update. /fix decision: Applied — `PARTIAL_MARKER_LINES` (the same first line, second line `PARTIAL_LINE`) is the partial body's answer. The pass prints "the partial write's own marker read back at the test question: yes/no". The verdict is `REPLACED` when the question list changed, `IGNORED` when every other question is untouched but the partial marker did not read back, and otherwise `MERGED` / `unclear`. Test: `test_a_partial_write_the_server_ignores_is_reported_ignored` (a 200 that applies nothing); the full run expects the partial marker in the partial PATCH. /fix notes: `scripts/probe-cliniko.py` (module docstring step 4 updated), `desktop/tests/test_probe_cliniko.py`; siblings: the restore's allowed set includes the partial marker (PR-MED-026). Verified: ruff and mypy clean in-leg; suite composer-run. /fix date: 2026-09-29. /fix applied by: Claude Code
- **PR-MED-028** (MED, behavioral, `scripts/probe-cliniko.py:845`): The positional-merge qualification does not independently cover both array levels. A target at `(0,1)` produces an unqualified `MERGED` even if sections are matched by position and questions by name; `(1,0)` likewise cannot distinguish questions matched by position within a named section. Either result leaves unsafe behavior for other target positions untested. — Evidence: the only positional blind-spot check is `("a merge by position", rich == FIRST)` (845), while `partial_content` places both the section and question at index zero (468). The positive test explicitly expects plain `MERGED` for section 1, question 2 (`desktop/tests/test_probe_cliniko.py:923`). Recommendation: Fix-now — Qualify section-position and question-position ambiguity separately, prefer a target displaced on both axes where available, and cover both mixed positional/name matching behaviors. /fix decision: Applied — `_blind_axes(position)` counts the untestable levels. `rich_target` takes the first of the least blind shared-section paragraphs (both indices ≥ 1 first). The `MERGED` qualifier names "sections matched by position" (target in section 0) and "questions matched by position" (question 0) separately, beside "a replace of the named section"; `FIRST` is removed. Tests:
  - `test_one_level_matched_by_position_is_reported_replaced[sections|questions]`: a `Mixed` server matching one level by position and the other by name, on a `(1,1)` target, gives REPLACED and MARKER LEFT last.
  - `test_a_fully_displaced_target_merged_by_name_is_plain_merged`.
  - `test_a_target_in_the_first_section_qualifies_sections_by_position` (`(0,1)`, replacing the plain-`MERGED` expectation the peer cited).
  - `test_a_target_first_in_its_section_qualifies_questions_by_position` (`(1,0)`).
  - A one-level `rich_target` case.
  - The full-run and lone-question expectations name both levels.

  /fix notes: `scripts/probe-cliniko.py` (module docstring selection and verdict text updated), `desktop/tests/test_probe_cliniko.py`; siblings none. Verified: ruff and mypy clean in-leg; suite composer-run. /fix date: 2026-09-29. /fix applied by: Claude Code
- Verification counts: 3 claims checked, 3 confirmed, 0 dropped as unverifiable
- Last reviewed: 2026-09-29

#### LEG 1 verified tuples (executor, 2026-09-29T10:25:55+10:00)
- PR-MED-026: materiality=behavioral severity=low surface=production rec=Fix-now — confirmed. `_restore` excludes the targets (`probe-cliniko.py:862-866`) and then PATCHes `original` (:877), which deviates from Flow 4's "only if the markers are the only difference" (plan :175). Downgraded to low because the targets are the probe's own throwaway questions on a DUMMY note, and restoring the original over them IS the goal. The normal path also routinely leaves non-marker text there. The full pass tells the practitioner to edit the rich-text test question (:792-794), the PATCH overwrites that in Cliniko but not in the open editor, and the restore pause (:856-858) has them save that editor, which writes the stale edit back. So a guard that REFUSES on non-marker target text would give MARKER LEFT on the ordinary run. Narrowed fix: keep restoring, but print for each test question whether it held anything besides the marker (a fixed line, no text) and that the restore replaced it. Add an AS-BUILT note to Flow 4 / Task 1.2 saying the restore condition is "structure unchanged and every UNTARGETED question as at the start". Test: a target edited during the restore pause restores and prints the line.
- PR-MED-027: materiality=behavioral severity=med surface=production rec=Fix-now — confirmed. The partial body re-sends the rich-text marker the full pass already installed: `partial_content(first, rich, targets[rich])` (:784) with the same `targets` as the full pass (:790). The verdict looks only at the OTHER questions (`if p != rich`, :831) before `MERGED` (:836). So a 200 that ignores the partial body is indistinguishable from a merge, and Task 2.1 would choose a body shape Cliniko drops, while D5 records a 200 as written. Fix: send a partial-pass marker distinguishable from the full pass's (a different second line under the same `MARKER_TEXT` first line, so `marker_present` and the MARKER LEFT paths are unchanged). Require it to read back at `rich` before `MERGED`, else print a new `IGNORED` verdict. Test with a server that answers the partial PATCH 200 without applying it.
- PR-MED-028: materiality=behavioral severity=med surface=production rec=Fix-now — confirmed, a same-family recurrence of round 11 MED-001 (that fix covered only the both-axes case `rich == FIRST`, :845). `partial_content` always sends the target as section 0, question 0 (:461-468), so a target in section 0 cannot catch sections matched by position, and a target at question 0 cannot catch questions matched by position within a named section. The fixture's only shared paragraph is `(0,0)`, and the positive test expects a plain `MERGED` at section 1, question 2 (`test_probe_cliniko.py:923`), which is section index 0 and therefore section-blind. Fix:
  - `rich_target` prefers a paragraph with section index ≥ 1 AND question index ≥ 1 in a shared section.
  - The verdict qualifies each untested axis separately ("sections by position" when `rich.section == 0`, "questions by position" when `rich.question == 0`).
  - Tests: a server matching sections by position and questions by name, and one matching sections by name and questions by position; each must give REPLACED/unclear for a fully displaced target, and a qualified `MERGED` for a target on the blind axis.
- Scope: every recommended fix changes only `scripts/probe-cliniko.py` (plus `desktop/tests/test_probe_cliniko.py`, and the plan's Flow 4 / Task 1.2 AS-BUILT note for PR-MED-026); no other production module.
- Cap verdict: accept — production-behavioral — all three are confined to the probe's partial-verdict and restore reporting in `scripts/probe-cliniko.py`, and are Fix-now in one leg; peer round 1 of cap 5 leaves room for the next codex round to confirm them.

### Round 17 - 2026-09-29 - Build-ahead (Tasks 3.1, 4.1, 5.1, 5.3) code, independent cross-family codex peer review (pass stage-3.p1)

- Round status: Closed (0 pending)
- Source: independent cross-family codex peer review
- Reviewer: codex gpt-6-astra (medium)
- Scope: Specified build-ahead tree diff and current scoped files; rendering parity, custody, microcopy, confinement and test honesty verified by reading only; no files written, tests run or network commands issued.
- **PR-MED-029** (MED, behavioral, `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note.py:976`): The section renderer can produce lines that its answer converter rejects, even for validated note data. Assertion text containing `\n` is returned unsplit by `_clean_lines`; passed prose containing `\r\n` retains `\r` after splitting. Passing either result through `render_targets` into `to_cliniko_answer` raises instead of producing an answer. Neither `NoteSpan` nor `StyleRendering` forbids those inputs. — Evidence: `note.py:925–928`: `text = assertion.text` followed by `lines.append(text)`; `note.py:976`: `lines = rendering.prose_text.split("\n")`; `draft_write.py:94–95`: `if any("\n" in line or "\r" in line for line in lines): raise ValueError(...)`. Recommendation: Fix-now — Split LF/CRLF/CR into actual lines for apparatus-free output, preserving blank lines and leaving Copy’s apparatus-enabled bytes unchanged; add renderer-to-answer composition tests. /fix decision: Applied (verified low) — `draft_write.render_targets` passes every apparatus-free line through the new `_as_lines` (`str.splitlines`, an empty line kept as one empty line), so a line break inside an assertion or a passed rendering (LF, CRLF, CR, or any other `splitlines` boundary) becomes separate lines with blank lines kept; `note.py` and Copy's `render_note` bytes are untouched (the frozen oracle still pins them); the docstring states the rule. Test: `test_draft_write.py::test_a_line_break_inside_a_rendered_line_becomes_lines_the_answer_accepts` — an assertion `"Left\r\nshoulder\rstrain\nsuspected"` and a passed rendering `"First.\r\nSecond.\rThird.\n\nFourth."` compose renderer → grouper → answer in both target types without raising, with the exact lines and rich-text answer pinned. Ruff and mypy clean; suite pending the composer's run. (2026-09-29, Claude Code)
- **PR-MED-030** (MED, test-harness, `desktop/tests/test_cliniko_client.py:1727`): The private-member exemption trusts the identifier `self`, allowing an ordinary static alias to bypass write confinement. Given a live call, `self = call; self._send('PATCH', '/treatment_notes/1', body)` produces no offender: `_send` is exempt on `self`, and `PATCH` is no longer a forbidden constant. Client subclass aliases also evade the bare-name subclass check. These are static routes beyond the documented dynamic-name residue, so the claimed confinement is not enforced. — Evidence: `on_self = isinstance(node.value, ast.Name) and node.value.id == "self"` and `or (node.attr in private and not on_self)`; subclass detection at line 1724 checks only `base.id in client_classes`. `cliniko_client.py:784` forwards `_send` to the transport. Recommendation: Fix-now — Replace the blanket `self` exemption with explicit allowances for legitimate owning classes and members, account for aliased client bases, and add negative samples for both bypasses. /fix decision: Applied (verified low) — the exemption is now STRUCTURAL (`_own_member_uses`): `<receiver>.<private>` is exempt only when the receiver is the enclosing method's FIRST parameter (a `staticmethod` has none), that parameter is not rebound anywhere in the method (`_where_parameter_holds`: an assignment, `del`, a loop, comprehension or walrus target voids the method's exemption; a nested function or lambda that shadows it, and a nested class, are skipped), and the enclosing class ITSELF defines the member (a method, a class-body binding, or a store on the first parameter) — so a module-level `self = call`, a rebound `self`, and a subclass's inherited `_send` all fail. The subclass rule reads every name inside a base expression: a client class by name, as an attribute (`encounter.ClinikoCall`), or through any chain of module-bound aliases (`_client_class_aliases`). Checked against the real tree by grep: every derived private name used outside the client (`_transport` in `ClinicRegistry`; `_clock` in `RateLimitLatch`, `VerificationLedger`, `ChromeBridge`, `PractitionerScreen`, `RecoveryScreen`, `SystemPauseWatch`, `ClinicRegistry`) is used in the class that assigns it, with no rebinding. Samples: ten new reaches (module-level and function-level `self = call`; a class using an undefined `_send`; a rebound `self`; a `staticmethod`; a loop-target `self`; `encounter.ClinikoCall`, `B = ClinikoCall`, a two-link alias chain, a subscripted base) and two class-context near-misses (a registry using its own members through a property, a lambda and past a shadowing nested function; a first parameter named `me`) replacing the old module-level `self._transport` / `self._clock` near-misses. The class docstring states the new rules. Ruff and mypy clean; suite pending the composer's run. (2026-09-29, Claude Code)
- Verification counts: 2 claims checked, 2 confirmed, 0 dropped as unverifiable
- Last reviewed: 2026-09-29

#### LEG 1 verified tuples (executor, 2026-09-29T11:55:18+10:00)
- PR-MED-029: materiality=behavioral severity=low surface=production rec=Fix-now — CONFIRMED at the type level. `NoteSpan.span_text` (`note.py:384`) and `StyleRendering.prose_text` (`note.py:659`) do not forbid a line break. `_clean_lines` keeps assertion text whole (`note.py:925-928`), and the prose branch splits on `"\n"` only (`note.py:976`), so the apparatus-free output of `render_section_lines` can carry `\n` or `\r`, which `to_cliniko_answer` refuses (`draft_write.py:94-95`). Downgraded to LOW for two reasons.
  - No known producer yields one. The prose is whitespace-collapsed (`prose_style.py:375`). Typed lines pass `_no_control_chars` (`ui/models.py:2224`). Config text does too (`note_config.py:218`). The transcript spans' words were not traced.
  - The failure is fail-closed: a `ValueError` before any request, so no wrong content can reach Cliniko.
  - Recommended fix: in `draft_write.render_targets`, split each apparatus-free line on universal newlines, keeping blank lines. `note.py` and Copy's bytes stay untouched, since the frozen oracle pins `render_note`. Add a renderer-to-answer composition test with LF, CRLF and CR in both an assertion and a passed rendering.
- PR-MED-030: materiality=behavioral severity=low surface=test-harness rec=Fix-now — CONFIRMED, in two parts.
  - The private-member exemption keys on the IDENTIFIER `self` (`test_cliniko_client.py:1727`, `:1732`), so `self = call; self._send(...)` passes. `PATCH` is no longer a forbidden constant after round 16.
  - The subclass rule reads only a bare-`Name` base (`:1724`), so `B = ClinikoCall; class X(B)` passes, and so does `class X(encounter.ClinikoCall)`, an `Attribute` base that `encounter` re-exports.
  - Both are STATIC routes, and the class docstring claims "only as a module's OWN `self.<name>`" and "no class subclasses a client class", so the claim over-reaches. The peer rated it MED; I rate it LOW. It needs a deliberate non-parameter `self` binding or a client-class alias, and there is none in `desktop/src` (grep `^\s*self\s*=` and `= ClinikoCall|ClinikoClient|HTTPSTransport`: no match). Production is unaffected.
  - Recommended fix, structural rather than another spelling: exempt `<receiver>.<private>` only when the receiver is the enclosing method's FIRST parameter, that parameter is never rebound in the method, AND the enclosing class itself defines that member (a `self.<name>` store or a `def <name>`). This covers `self = call` and a subclass through an alias, since the subclass never defines `_send`. Refuse ANY `ClassDef` base that resolves to a client class, whether through an `Attribute` whose `attr` is a client class name or through a module-level `Name` bound to one. Add negative samples for all three routes.
- Scope check: neither fix leaves this stage's files or needs a blocked task's code. PR-MED-029's fix goes in `draft_write.py` (Task 3.1) plus its test. PR-MED-030's fix goes in `desktop/tests/test_cliniko_client.py::TestWriteCallSites` (Task 5.3).
- Cap verdict: accept — production-behavioral — both findings verify as LOW. PR-MED-029 fails closed before any request and no known producer yields a line break. PR-MED-030 needs a deliberate non-parameter `self` or a client-class alias, and `desktop/src` has none. Each Fix-now is a small in-scope change that the next peer confirmation round can read.

PEER-ROUND-17 RESULT: 2 findings (CRIT 0 / HIGH 0 / MED 2 / LOW 0).

For successive assertion texts `"First.\n"` and `"Second."`, the renderer yields `["First.\n", "Second."]`, but the grouper produces `["First.", "Second."]`; the blank paragraph is lost. The new composition test covers interior blanks only. — Evidence: `"return [part for line in lines for part in (line.splitlines() or [line])]"`; `note.py:928`: `"lines.append(text)"`; `test_draft_write.py:165` uses `"First.\r\nSecond.\rThird.\n\nFourth."`. Recommendation: Fix-now — Preserve trailing empty segments when normalising LF/CRLF/CR and add composition cases with a trailing break followed by another assertion; also correct `to_cliniko_answer`’s stale claim at line 101 that `render_section_lines` never yields embedded breaks. /fix decision: Applied (verified low) — `draft_write._as_lines` appends the empty line after a TRAILING break (when `line.splitlines(keepends=True)[-1]` differs from the last split part), so `"First.\n"` then `"Second."` gives `["First.", "", "Second."]` — the blank line Copy shows; `render_targets`' docstring says every blank line is kept, the trailing one included, and `to_cliniko_answer`'s stale claim is replaced by what is true (`render_section_lines` can yield a line holding a break; `render_targets` splits it first). Test: `test_draft_write.py::test_a_trailing_line_break_keeps_its_blank_line` — assertions `"First.\n"`, `"Second."`, `"Third.\r\n"` give `["First.", "", "Second.", "Third.", ""]` and the matching rich-text answer (a `"\n"`-only assertion is refused by `NoteSpan`, so it is not a case). Ruff and mypy clean; suite pending the composer's run. (2026-09-29, Claude Code) **Follow-up (leg f18b, after the composer's run: 1 failed, 4463 passed):** the per-line trailing rule broke round 17's CRLF case — `render_section_lines`' prose branch splits on `"\n"` alone, so `"First.\r\nSecond."` arrives as the line `"First.\r"`, whose trailing CR the per-line rule read as a break with a blank line after it (`['First.', '', 'Second.', …]`). The test's expectation was RIGHT (a CRLF is one break) and is unchanged; the fix: `_as_lines` now joins the lines with `"\n"` exactly as `render_note` does and splits the joined text ONCE on every line boundary, keeping the empty line after a trailing break — so the write's lines are exactly the lines Copy's text shows, a split CRLF stays one break, and both round-17 and round-18 composition tests hold. Ruff and mypy clean; suite pending the composer's run. (2026-09-29, Claude Code)
- **PR-LOW-032** (LOW, test-harness, `desktop/tests/test_cliniko_client.py:1725`): The structural exemption misses pattern-capture rebinding because capture names are stored as strings on pattern nodes, not `ast.Name` stores. A class defining `_send = None` can contain `def f(self, call):` followed by `match call: case self: self._send('PATCH', '/treatment_notes/1', body)`; the capture replaces the receiver with `call`, yet `_client_reach` exempts the private access. This contradicts the new “never rebound” guarantee and is outside the documented dynamic-name residue. — Evidence: rebinding detection is only `"isinstance(node, ast.Name) and node.id == name and not isinstance(node.ctx, ast.Load)"`; exemption requires `"node.attr in defined"` and `"node.value.id == first"` at lines 1776–1778; the docstring claims `"method's first parameter, never rebound in that method"` at line 1876. Recommendation: Fix-now — Account structurally for bindings represented outside `ast.Name`, including pattern captures, and add a negative sample through `_client_reach`. /fix decision: Applied (verified low) — structural, per LEG 1: the new `_binds_again(fn, name)` asks the compiler — the stdlib `symtable` of `ast.unparse(fn)`, the method's own `Function` table found breadth-first — whether the parameter is `is_assigned()` or `is_imported()` in the method's OWN scope, so every binding form the language has voids the exemption (a nested scope's binding lives in its own child table; source the compiler refuses on its own counts as bound — fail closed). `_where_parameter_holds` asks it first and keeps its AST walk for the shadowing skips and comprehension targets; `_own_member_uses` runs it only for methods that touch a private client name on their first parameter. Samples: thirteen rebindings in a class that DOES define `_send` — `match` captures (`case self`, `[*self]`, `{**self}`, `{'k': self}`), `except … as self`, `import os as self`, `from os import path as self`, a nested `def self` / `class self`, `with … as self`, `del self`, an annotated and an augmented assignment — each must reach, and the same class with no rebinding must not (the control). The class docstring names the compiler as what enforces "never bound again". Ruff and mypy clean; suite pending the composer's run. (2026-09-29, Claude Code)

Use the stdlib `symtable` of the module source, find the method's table (name + line), and void the exemption when `lookup(first).is_assigned()` or `is_imported()` in that table. That covers every binding form the language has, and the named ones above become self-test samples. A nested scope's own binding lives in its own child table, so the shadowing skip stays as it is.

- **PR-LOW-033** (LOW, test-harness, `desktop/tests/test_cliniko_client.py:1733`): A nested function can rebind the method’s receiver through `nonlocal` and a pattern capture while retaining the private-member exemption. In a class defining `_send = None`, `f(self, call)` can define and invoke `replace()` containing `nonlocal self` and `match call: case self: pass`, then call `self._send(...)`. The outer symbol table does not mark this child-scope assignment as its own assignment; the subsequent AST walk misses the string-valued capture. Consequently `_client_reach` admits the rebound receiver. — Evidence: `_binds_again` returns `"symbol.is_assigned() or symbol.is_imported()"` for the method’s table; the remaining walk at line 1755 detects only `"isinstance(node, ast.Name) and node.id == name and not isinstance(node.ctx, ast.Load)"`. Recommendation: Fix-now — Account for nested scopes that declare the receiver nonlocal and bind it, and add this executable source shape as a negative sample through `_client_reach`, retaining the unmodified-receiver control. /fix decision: Applied (verified low) — still the compiler's answer: `_binds_again`, after the method's own table, walks EVERY descendant table (functions, lambdas, comprehensions, class bodies, at any depth) and counts the parameter rebound when any of them holds the name `is_nonlocal()` AND (`is_assigned()` or `is_imported()`); a `nonlocal` that only reads it does not count. The docstring says so. Samples through `_client_reach` in a class that DOES define `_send`: five new nested-scope rebindings — the peer's `nonlocal self` + `case self` in a called nested function, `nonlocal` + `except … as self`, `nonlocal` + `import os as self`, a nested class body's `nonlocal` + `from os import path as self`, and a two-levels-deep `nonlocal` + `def self` — each must reach; two controls must not: no rebinding, and a nested function with `nonlocal self` that only returns it. Ruff and mypy clean; suite pending the composer's run. (2026-09-29, Claude Code)

### Round 28 - 2026-09-29 - Phase 3 write orchestration (Tasks 3.2-3.4, profile correction), independent cross-family codex peer review (pass stage-3.p2, slice A)

- Round status: Closed (0 pending)
- Source: independent cross-family codex peer review
- Reviewer: codex gpt-6-astra (medium)
- Scope: Slice A’s specified diff and current implementation, permitted plan sections and callee context; static reading only, no writes, tests or network.
- **PR-MED-036** (MED, behavioral, `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/draft_write.py:1310`): An open attempt can become retryable when the current profile stops matching a previously written target. For example, after an unknown diagnosis-only write lands, remapping the diagnosis section to another empty question leaves the original answer outside the current match. Digest confirmation fails, but retry checks only the new match and permits another write, duplicating the clinical content without resolving the earlier attempt. The saved-note identity remains unchanged. — Evidence: line 494 skips targets when `"target.target_id not in mapped"`; line 864 compares `"current.get(target_id) == digest"`; line 888 falls through with `"return None"`; line 1310 calls `"retry_verdict(hop1.content, baseline, match)"` without the recorded target set. Recommendation: Fix-now — Refuse an open attempt as `write_uncertain` when any recorded target cannot be reconciled; bind recorded targets to their question mapping so remapping an existing target ID also fails closed, and add a profile-change regression case. /fix decision: Applied (2026-09-29T17:30:04+10:00, executor leg f28) — `draft_write.match_digest(match, target_ids)`: the SHA-256 of each written target's (section name, question name, representation), sorted by id; None when a target is not in the match. `WriteRecord` gains the required `match_sha256` (labels hashed, so it stays ids and digests only; no production writer before Task 5.2, so there is nothing to migrate); `PreparedWrite` carries it; `attempt_record` copies it. `confirm_written` now refuses an OPEN record whose `match_digest` under the current match differs from the recorded one (a target unmapped or moved) as `Uncertain` → `write_uncertain`, before the digest confirmation and so before the retry verdict. A `refused` record is unaffected (Cliniko applied nothing). The docstrings of `confirm_written`, `prepare_write` step 3, `WriteRecord` and `PreparedWrite` say so. Tests (`test_draft_write.py`): `test_an_open_attempt_under_a_changed_profile_is_write_uncertain` covers both ways in, section re-pointed (target unmapped) and target moved to another question, for `attempting` and `unknown`, plus the non-vacuity leg (a refused record under the changed profile still prepares). `test_the_match_digest_names_where_targets_are_written` pins order-insensitivity, subset, absent → None and moved → a different digest. The record key-set pin and the malformed-field case gain `match_sha256`; `_record` records the default match's digest. `test_session_machine.py`'s `_write_record` fixture gains the field. Files: `draft_write.py`, `test_draft_write.py`, `test_session_machine.py` (all Phase 3).
- **PR-LOW-037** (LOW, test-harness, `desktop/tests/test_draft_write.py:1668`): The discovery-path test does not establish that the eventual PATCH uses the final note read’s content. Its fake repeats the same answer for both note GETs, and its assertions check request order and template matching only. Returning discovery content alongside final verification would therefore escape this coverage, potentially losing an intervening checkbox edit or overwriting newly typed text. The current implementation correctly uses `final.content`; this is a missing regression guard. — Evidence: line 1668 constructs `"cliniko = Cliniko()"`; lines 1672–1677 assert the request sequence and `"store.reads == 1 and hop1.template_matches"`. The end-to-end test at line 2058 supplies `TEMPLATE`, bypassing discovery. Recommendation: Fix-now — Queue distinct discovery and final note bodies, then verify through preparation and send that final-read untargeted edits survive and final-read owned text refuses PATCH. /fix decision: Applied (2026-09-29T17:30:04+10:00, executor leg f28) — `TestHopOne::test_the_final_read_not_the_discovery_read_builds_the_write`. The discovery and final note reads answer differently. The final read's cleared consent box reaches `hop1.content`, the prepared body and the PATCH as sent. Typed text in the Diagnosis question on the final read only refuses `note_has_text`. Test file only.
- Verification counts: 4 claims checked, 2 confirmed, 2 dropped as unverifiable
- Last reviewed: 2026-09-29

#### LEG 1 verified tuples (executor, 2026-09-29T17:25:59+10:00)
- PR-MED-036: materiality=behavioral severity=med surface=production rec=Fix-now — confirmed: `WriteRecord` (`draft_write.py:733-753`) binds only `target_ids` + digests, never the question each target was written to. `confirm_written` → `_digests_match` (:861-865) reads the CURRENT match's positions via `answer_digests` (:656-675). `retry_verdict` (:891-897) → `note_has_text` covers only the current match's questions. So a profile change between an open attempt and the retry lets a second write through, and the full body (`build_body` :684-710) keeps the old question's landed text: a duplicate in the draft. Two ways: a recorded target unmapped (:494 skips it) or remapped to another question. D4 binds the profile against the CURRENT config at the click (`bind_template_profile`, note_config.py:2741), so a user-override edit or an upgrade between attempts in one session (Unreviewed / recovery survive restarts) is the trigger. MED, not HIGH: it needs a config change inside an open attempt's session, and the result is a DRAFT the clinician reviews before finalising.
- PR-LOW-037: materiality=behavioral severity=low surface=test-harness rec=Fix-now — confirmed: `test_no_template_id_reads_the_note_to_discover_it` (`test_draft_write.py:1666-1677`) builds `Cliniko()`, whose note route repeats ONE answer (`Cliniko.request` :601-607), and asserts only the request order and `template_matches`. Production correctly takes `content=final.content` (`draft_write.py:1131`); no test would catch the discovery read's content being used instead. A missing regression guard, test file only.
- Scope: both fixes stay inside Phase 3's files (`draft_write.py`, `test_draft_write.py`), and neither needs Task 4.2 / 5.2 code. PR-MED-036's fix needs NO practitioner choice. It is a refusal by D5's existing rule: an open attempt that cannot be reconciled is `write_uncertain`, whose line already exists and is prefixed; no new wording. Recommended shape:
  - `WriteRecord` gains a digest of the match it was written under, each recorded target → (section name, question name, representation). This is ids and digests only, so D5's content-free rule holds. `write.enc` has no production writer before Task 5.2, so no existing record needs migrating.
  - An open attempt whose recorded targets are not all in the current match, or whose match digest differs, is `Uncertain` → `write_uncertain`, before the digest confirmation and the retry verdict.
  - Plus a regression test for each way in (unmapped, remapped).
- Cap verdict: accept — production-behavioral — one MED with a closed-form fail-closed fix under D5's existing `write_uncertain` rule and one LOW test guard, both inside Phase 3's two files; peer_round 1 of 5 leaves room for the confirmation round.

### Round 35 - 2026-09-29 - Phase 5 write wiring (Tasks 5.2, 5.3), independent cross-family codex peer review (pass stage-5.p1, slice A)

- Round status: Closed (0 pending)
- Source: independent cross-family codex peer review
- Reviewer: codex gpt-6-astra (medium)
- Scope: Phase 5 write wiring and confinement against the supplied tree diff, current scoped files and permitted plan sections; static inspection only, with no writes, tests or network commands.
- **PR-MED-038** (MED, behavioral, `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/main_window.py:1822`): A cooldown recorded by the Chrome bridge while hop 1 runs does not prevent the subsequent PATCH. The click checks the latch initially, but the between-hop handler checks only whether its own result was rate-limited. A concurrent bridge verification can therefore establish the shared cooldown before this handler dispatches a new write request. — Evidence: the initial check is `cooling = latch.cooling(context.clinic_id, latch.clock())` at line 1702; between hops, `if result.rate_limited:` is the only cooldown branch before `_store_write_record(job, attempt)` and hop-2 dispatch. `ui/bridge.py:831` independently calls `self._latch.record_429(result.request.clinic.clinic_id, self._latch.clock())`. Recommendation: Fix-now — Recheck the shared latch before recording and dispatching a new attempt, preserve the uncertainty prefix, and add a gated-hop test where another producer starts the cooldown; reconcile D13’s “once per click” wording with this guard. /fix decision: Applied (verified low; 2026-09-29T19:41:36+10:00, Claude Code executor) — `ui/main_window.py` `_prepare_attempt` reads `latch.cooling` again after the hop-1 429 branch and before the session check, the default-source read and any record store; a cooling clinic returns the existing `rate_limited` line with the click's `uncertain` prefix, nothing stored, the release through `_end_write`; the docstring names it. Test `test_ui_encounter.py::TestDraftWrite::test_a_cooldown_another_path_records_during_hop_one_stops_hop_two` (parametrised with and without an earlier open attempt): the note GET held at a gate, `record_429` on the shared latch, then the gate released — the line, two GETs and no PATCH, nothing stored, one release. #### Issues Found

- **[LOW] LOW-001** `draft_write.py` `prepare_write` (the per-target digest loop) — reconcile's two halves assume that for every written target the expected-final digest differs from the before digest: an open record whose current answers equal `digests` reads `written` (no PATCH) BEFORE the before-digest check runs, so a target whose appended answer normalises to the answer as read would make an attempt that never landed reconcile as `written`. That happens only when the answer as read hides what follows it (e.g. an unterminated comment or quoted attribute — markup the parser reads leniently, not markup Cliniko's sanitiser is known to store); seen-mode completion still makes the clinician look before Complete. Pattern siblings: none (`prepare_write` is the one builder of `digests` / `before_digests`; searched `before_digests`, `answer_digest(`). Invariant: every `PreparedWrite` has `digests[t] != before_digests[t]` for every `t` in `target_ids`. Triage: Fix-now; Decision: Applied — `prepare_write` refuses `note_unreadable` when a target's `expected == before` (the D15 refusal for "an answer the app cannot append to"), with its docstring step 6 and the module docstring's enforced list updated; tests: `test_an_append_that_reads_as_the_answer_before_it_refuses` (deterministic — `appended_answer` stubbed to keep a typed answer unchanged → `note_unreadable`) and `test_no_prepared_target_has_equal_final_and_before_digests` (the invariant over two real hiding answers, whichever way the parser version reads them) (2026-09-30, Claude Code).
- **[LOW] LOW-002** `ui/main_window.py` `_prepare_attempt` docstring — Task 7.1's edit left a ragged reflow ("a\n refusal, or\n ``written_seen`` …"). Triage: Fix-now; Decision: Applied — reflowed (2026-09-30, Claude Code).

Behaviour: Flow 4 exactly — inputs (email, note URL, key by `getpass`, the dummy patient's surname, the note id's last four digits; surname and digits compared to the re-read note and never printed); refusals unless `draft: true`, `finalized_at` null, not archived, patient = URL's, practitioner = key user's; what-it-will-do summary and a typed `yes`; the adversarial marker into ONE rich-text and ONE plain-text question chosen by printed position; re-read and per-question `equal` / `sanitised` / `rejected` plus untargeted-unchanged (questions 1, 2, 4); the template structure and default-text presence (question 3); the editor pause and marker-survival report (question 5); a SECOND marker pass that pauses BETWEEN its note GET and its PATCH ("now edit and save a different question in the open editor, then press Enter"), PATCHes the full re-read body, re-reads and reports whether that edit survived (question 6 — a `--partial` variant of the same pass sends only the targeted question and reports whether the untargeted edit and the other questions survived, answering question 2 as merge vs replace); restore only if the markers are the only difference, else `MARKER LEFT - delete it by hand in Cliniko` and exit 2 (AS-BUILT, codex round 12: the restore condition is Flow 4's AS-BUILT one — structure unchanged and every UNTARGETED question as at the start, with a per-test-question report (PR-MED-026); the partial pass writes its own marker and reports `IGNORED` when it does not read back (PR-MED-027); the question-2 verdict names each array level the target cannot test (PR-MED-028)). **AS-BUILT (smoke 2026-09-29, practitioner decision "Probe skips it"):** the plain-text target is OPTIONAL. A note with no `text` question is tested on its rich-text question alone. The run prints that it gives no plain-text results, and the prompts name "the test question". Every other step and safety property is unchanged: the refusals, the typed `yes`, the fresh read, structure-only stdout, restore-or-`MARKER LEFT`. A checkboxes answer is checked as one of the other questions throughout. A note with no `paragraph` question is refused. `--test-write-final` already falls back from rich-text to plain-text and is unchanged. The `--test-write-final` leg refuses unless the note re-reads as final, PATCHes the marker, prints the status and the filtered 422 names; a 200 is printed as an alarm with the same instruction. Structure only in stdout. Acceptance: Validation's Probe bullet.

### Phase 2 — The practitioner's test write and the decision it feeds (no code)
- [x] 🟩 **Task P.1 — The practitioner runs the test write on clinic 1** (practitioner-owned; a normal terminal at the repo root; a note the practitioner opens on a DUMMY patient they create in their own clinic; the finalised leg on a second note of the same dummy patient, finalised by hand first). Done-when: the Done note answers all SIX questions with the printed structure pasted (for question 3 / Option C: the practitioner transcribes the scaffold text of every defaulted question from a PRISTINE note as Cliniko's editor shows it into `template_defaults.json`, keyed by the clinic's host (from the address bar) and the template's name as Cliniko shows it; the probe's output names the questions only), plus the finalised leg's status and categories. Clinic 2 stays deferred.
  - **Done (2026-09-29, clinic 1; practitioner-pasted probe output, structure only — no id, URL, name or answer text recorded).** Preceded by the batched Phase 1 smoke, steps 1–5 PASS (the stricter transport carries Validate / Replace key, the Chrome note check, the checkout re-verification and the read-only probe). The first `--test-write` run refused before writing (the template has no plain-text question); the probe was fixed to run rich-text-only (practitioner decision "Probe skips it", codex round 21 clean) and re-run on the same dummy patient's open draft.
    - **Printed structure.** Template "Standard Consultation", 3 sections of 1 / 3 / 3 questions: s1 q1 `paragraph` (the History question, carrying the template's default text); s2 q1 `paragraph` (Assessment), q2 `checkboxes` (Informed Consent, an `answers` list), q3 `paragraph` (Diagnosis — the test question); s3 q1–q3 `paragraph` (Treatment/Management). No `text` question.
    - **Q1 (how Cliniko stores an answer):** the rich-text marker came back `sanitised` — its bytes changed (the adversarial `<`, `&` and quotes rewritten) but its visible text survived one HTML decode, which is D4's normalised comparison. Plain text: not testable (no `text` question in the template).
    - **Q2 (partial vs full body):** `REPLACED`. A PATCH carrying one question replaced the note's ENTIRE content — the other question's edit lost, every other question gone, the note left with one section and one question (the marker landed as s1 q1, not at the test position). Cliniko does not merge partial `content`.
    - **Q3 (does the template GET carry default text):** yes. The TEMPLATE GET carries the default answer text for s1 q1 (as the question's `answer`) and `choices` for the checkboxes question, so Option A is available.
    - **Q4 (untargeted questions after a full-body write):** every other question byte-identical: yes, and visibly identical: yes — the checkboxes answer and the History default text included.
    - **Q5 (the open Cliniko editor):** the marker was still present after the practitioner saved the ALREADY-OPEN editor: **no**. An open editor's save OVERWRITES the API write.
    - **Q6 (the full-body GET→PATCH window):** an edit to another question AND to the test question, both saved in the editor between the probe's GET and its PATCH: after the full-body PATCH **neither edit survived**. A full body reverts an untargeted edit saved in the window as well as a targeted one.
    - **Restore:** refused as designed ("structure unchanged since the start: no"), `MARKER LEFT` printed last. The partial write had broken the dummy note, and the practitioner archives it. Nothing outside that dummy note was touched.
    - **Finalised leg (`--test-write-final`)**, on a second note of the same dummy patient, finalised by hand (its URL copied from the edit page before finalising; Cliniko opens a finalised note only from the patient's notes list): `PATCH /treatment_notes/<id> (finalised note): CredentialsRejected (HTTP 403)`, "Nothing was written." No 422 categories. The SAME key had just written the draft in the main leg, so this 403 is Cliniko refusing a write to a finalised note, not a key problem. Task 2.1 reads it that way.
    - **Clinic 2:** still deferred (no API-key permission on that account).
- [x] 🟩 **Task 2.1 — Decide the typed-text default source, the body shape and the completion mode** `[decision]`
  - Options (default source, D4): A — the template GET's default answer text (preferred if question 3 shows the template carries it); C — a per-clinic `template_defaults.json` written by the practitioner from P.1's pristine-note structure (Schema / Data Changes). A Start-time snapshot is NOT an option (PR-HIGH-001).
  - Options (body shape, D4): partial — only the targeted sections/questions (preferred if question 2 shows Cliniko MERGES partial content: an UNTARGETED edit saved during the GET→PATCH window cannot be reverted); full — the re-read content with targeted answers replaced. In BOTH modes a targeted-question edit in the window is replaced (PR-MED-011) — the residue is recorded in Task 6.1 whichever is chosen.
  - Options (completion, D6): auto — `written` completes at once (only if question 5 shows the open editor cannot overwrite our draft); seen — the Note tab stays with Copy and the "press Complete once you can see it" line, and Complete runs `complete_after_write`.
  - Decide after: Task P.1's Done note.
  - Blocks: Tasks 3.2, 3.3, 3.4, 4.2, 5.2.
  - **Chosen (2026-09-29, the practitioner, through the composer's AskUserQuestion on the P.1 results; answers quoted verbatim in the handoff note):**
    - **Body shape: FULL** ("Full note (Recommended)"): read the note, fill the scribe's questions and send the whole note back. Rationale: P.1 Q2 — a partial body REPLACES the whole note; Q4 — every untargeted question, the checkboxes answer and the default text included, round-trips byte-identical under a full body. Accepted residue (Q6): an edit saved in the Cliniko editor between the click's note GET and its PATCH is reverted, whether or not it is in a targeted question.
    - **Completion: SEEN** ("After you've seen it (Recommended)"): the Note tab keeps Copy and the reload-then-Complete line (`written_seen`), and Complete runs `complete_after_write`. Rationale: P.1 Q5 — saving an already-open Cliniko editor overwrites the API write, so auto-completion could destroy the only local copy of a note Cliniko no longer holds.
    - **Default source: a PER-CLINIC SETTING** ("Make it an option in the settings. So if people want to add their own one they can or they just use an existing cliniko template"; clarified: "A file, per clinic (Recommended)"). On the Clinics tab each clinic chooses **"Cliniko template"** (the default; Option A, the template GET's default answer text) or **"My own defaults"** (Option C, a per-clinic file the practitioner edits by hand, like `section_cues.json`, checked by the app, which names any question it cannot match). An in-app editor is out of scope (later). Rationale: P.1 Q3 shows the template GET carries the default text, so Option A works with no setup; Option C lets a practitioner whose drafts start from other text declare it. New work: D14, Tasks 3.1a and 5.4.
    - **A PATCH 403 reads as "the note was finalised"** ("Note was finalised (Recommended)"): a 403 on the write, just after the worker's GET verified the note with the same key, reads as "the note was finalised in Cliniko before the write reached it; the draft was not written; copy the note instead", not as a rejected key. Rationale: P.1's finalised leg — the key that had just written the draft got a 403 on a finalised note. A 401, and a 403 on either GET, stay `key_rejected` (D5).
  - Amendments recorded 2026-09-29 by the stage-2 executor: D2, D4, D5, D6, D9, the new D14, Schema / Data Changes, Config, Critical Constraints 4 and 13, Validation, the Planning Extraction Summary, External / API Findings, Flow 1, and Tasks 3.2, 3.3, 3.4, 4.2, 5.1, 5.2, 6.1 and P.2. New tasks 3.1a and 5.4 were hardened by the scoped `/review-plan` in round 22.

Acceptance: Validation's Custody bullet, remaining items. **AS-BUILT (stage-4, 2026-09-29):** (1) the controller gains a SECOND read, `referenced_session_ids()` (every id the ref registry names), beside `live_session_ids()` — `prune_session_refs` cannot enumerate the private registry otherwise; both are on `SessionControllerLike`. (2) `prune_session_refs()` is called as the LAST line of `prune_reminders` (its key-existence rule unchanged), not from `app.py`'s sweep, so the only caller stays the one `app.py` already has. (3) `complete_after_write` also refuses while any OTHER custody reservation is held (`_custody_reservations != {id: 1}`, `complete()`'s "a discard is completing" shape) and while a live worker is uncleared; a write held for another id — or a stale token beside a held one — is `WriteInFlightError`, no held token at all is `SessionActivityError`. It keeps the reservation on every refusal or failure; the CALLER releases it (`TranscriptScreen._complete_after_write`'s `finally`, a no-op once consumed). (4) The Complete routing reads `write_record_status` at the click: outcome `written` with `note_matches` → `complete_written`; `written` WITHOUT it (another saved note, or one that cannot be read) → refused with "Complete refused: " + `write_uncertain` before anything is reserved (round 31 LOW-002; `complete_after_write`'s own refusal stays the enforcing check); a failed status read completes nothing (fail closed); a recovered source never reads the record. The post-Save "delete note and complete without one" is refused by `_write_pending()`'s line once any attempt exists (round 31 LOW-001 — D5's freeze). (5) Task 5.2's clause "`_on_transcript_closed` treats `written` as completed and sets `written_done` after `refresh()`" was BUILT here — it is D6's own behaviour and needs no 5.2 code. Only the `written_done` line was needed: an adopted session's source is `"live"` and its reminder entry goes at adoption, so the recovered-source tuple is unchanged (round 31 LOW-005).

The original step list follows for the record. (practitioner-owned; scripted by the composer at the phase boundary; screenshots are the observation channel; each step names the exact on-screen line from Task 5.1). Steps: one consultation note written and reloaded in Chrome, then Complete pressed once it shows there (seen mode — expect `written_seen`, then `written_done`); the Clinics tab:
    - switch clinic 1 to "My own defaults" with no file — expect `CLINIC_DEFAULTS_MISSING` on "Check file" and `defaults_unreadable` on Write;
    - create the file for "Standard Consultation", transcribing the History question's default EXACTLY as Cliniko's editor shows it in a fresh note, per `docs/own-template-defaults.md` (otherwise the write is refused `note_has_text`), then "Check file" — expect `CLINIC_DEFAULTS_OK`;
    - add an entry for a question the template lacks and click Write — expect `defaults_unmatched` naming it "<question> in <section>" (R22-12);
    - remove that entry and write one note under "My own defaults";
    - switch back to "Cliniko template".

  - Validate and Replace key with an empty key — expect the `KEY_MISSING` line;
  - confirm or revise Task 5.1(e)'s `check_refused` wording for permanent note refusals (open a finalised note's recording and click Write);
  - a refused write on a note with typed text (type one word in Cliniko and save first); an unknown-outcome rehearsal (R22-16: hop 1 and hop 2 are under a second apart, so switching Wi-Fi off after the click usually fails HOP 1 — `check_failed`, no attempt — rather than giving `unknown`; either outcome passes the step, and the tests carry the `unknown` path; disable Wi-Fi after the click, reconnect, click again — expect a reconcile, no second draft); close refused mid-write; the Unreviewed path. The original step list follows. (practitioner-owned; after Phase 7's reviews; screenshots are the observation channel):
  1. Clinics tab: clinic 1 still listed and checked; no "Starting text" group.
  2. A fresh dummy draft holding only the template's prompts: record from Chrome, Save, Write, reload (F5) — the prompts kept, ONE empty line, then the app's text; Template and Appointment filled after F5; Informed Consent untouched. (Checks whether Cliniko keeps the empty line — record what it shows.)
  3. A dummy draft where you type "L knee pain" / "happened 2 months ago" in Presenting complaint and save in Cliniko: record, Save, Write, F5 — your two lines, an empty line, the app's text.

  5. Connection drop: Write, Wi-Fi off at once, reconnect, Write again — ONE copy of the text after F5.

## Follow-Up Continuation Notes
- Next follow-up first: PLAN.md Phase 6 (the audit record) — it reads the write outcome that today dies with `write.enc`; and clinic 2's P.1 + test write + smoke when its key exists.
- Stays out of scope there: POST of a second note, a Chrome write state, automatic finalisation. (Appending to a filled note moved INTO scope by D15, 2026-09-30.)
- Decisions that still apply: D1–D15 here (D8 and D14 superseded by D15) and the safeguards plan's D2/D4/D9/D10-as-amended/D11/D13.
- Must not be rediscovered: the write's own note GET is its verification (never the checkout's or the bridge's result); the `attempting` state is on disk before the PATCH is dispatched; a 200 is never relabelled; the reconcile compares per-question normalised digests, never a whole-body hash; the only copy after completion is in Cliniko; an already-open Cliniko editor's save DOES overwrite a PATCHed draft (P.1 Q5, hence seen mode); a partial `content` REPLACES the whole note (P.1 Q2, hence the full body); a PATCH 403 means the note was finalised (D5); the typed-text default source is per clinic (D14); the venv `python.exe` is a launcher; `urllib` bypasses the host pin; no Cliniko call at startup or idle keeps the no-sockets legs at zero.
